This is to inform you that the 19th Annual General Meeting of Members of the Company ( “AGM”) 
is scheduled to be held on Wednesday, September 16, 2026  at 11.00 A.M.  (IST) through Video 
Conferencing (“ VC”) / Other Audio-Visual Means (“ OAVM”) in accordance with the relevant 
circulars issued by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs and Securities and Exchange Board of India 
from time to time. 
 
In terms of the provisions of Regulation 34 of SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulations, 2015, as amended, please find enclosed herewith Annual Report for the 
financial year 2025-26 alongwith Notice convening the AGM, which are being sent through 
electronic mode to those Members whose email addresses are registered with the Company/Registrar 
and Share Transfer Agent/Depositories. Further, a letter providing the web-link to access the Notice 
of AGM and Annual Report is being sent to those Members who have not registered their email 
address. 
 
The Annual Report for the financial year 2025-26 along with the Notice of AGM is also available on 
the website of the Company at www.travelfoodservices.com . 
 
Kindly take the same on your record. 
 
Thanking You,

issued in this regard (“SEBI Circulars”) (collectively 
referred to as “Circulars”), permitted companies to 
conduct their Annual General Meeting through VC or 
OAVM without the physical presence of Members at a 
common venue. In accordance with the Circulars and in 
compliance with the provisions of the Companies Act, 
2013 read with Rules framed thereunder (“Act”) and the 
SEBI	(Listing	Obligations	 and	Disclosure	 Requirements)	
Regulations, 2015 (“SEBI Listing Regulations” ), the 19th 
Annual General Meeting (“AGM”) of the Company is being 
conducted through VC/OAVM. As the AGM will be held 
through	VC/OAVM,	 the	requirement	 of	providing	 a	route	
map to the venue is dispensed with, and accordingly, the 
same has not been annexed to this Notice.
2. There is no Special Business to be considered at the AGM 
and accordingly the Explanatory Statement pursuant 
to Section 102 of the Act and Secretarial Standard 
on General Meetings (SS-2) issued by the Institute of 
Company Secretaries of India, is not annexed hereto.
3. The deemed venue for the AGM shall be the Registered 
Office of the Company situated at Block-A, South Wing, 
1st Floor, Shiv Sagar Estate, Dr. Annie Besant Road, Worli, 
Mumbai - 400018.
4. Pursuant to the provisions of the Act, a Member entitled 
to attend and vote at the AGM is entitled to appoint a 
proxy to attend and vote on his/ her behalf and the 
proxy need not be a Member of the Company. Since 
this AGM is being held through VC / OAVM pursuant to

Dividend related Information:
15. The Board of Directors has recommended a final dividend 
of	₹	10.25	per	equity	share	of	face	value	₹	1	each	for	the	
financial year ended March 31, 2026, subject to approval 
of the Members at the AGM.
16. The Company has fixed Wednesday, September 9, 2026  
as the Record Date for determining the entitlement of 
Members to receive the said dividend.
17. The dividend, if declared at the AGM, shall be paid within 
the statutory timelines to those Members whose names 
appear in the Register of Members/Beneficial Owners as 
on the Record Date.
18. Members are informed that, dividend payments will be 
made based on the bank account details registered with 
their	respective	 DPs.	Members	 are	therefore	 requested	 to	
ensure that their bank particulars and address details are 
updated with their DPs as the Company and its RTA will 
not	be	able	to	act	upon	requests	 received	 directly	from	
Members. Members who wish to update their PAN, KYC, 
Bank	details	and	Nomination,	 are	requested	 to	contact	
their respective DPs.
Information related to Tax Deducted at Source (“TDS”) on 
Dividend
19. Pursuant to the provisions of the Income Tax Act, 2025 
(“IT Act”), dividend income is taxable in the hands of 
the	Members	 and	the	Company	 is	required	 to	deduct	
tax at source (“TDS”) on the dividend paid, at the rates 
prescribed under the IT Act or under the applicable Double 
Taxation Avoidance Agreement, as the case may be.
20. To enable the Company to comply with the applicable 
TDS	provisions,	 Members	 are	requested	 to	ensure	that	
their residential status, Permanent Account Number 
(PAN) (duly linked with Aadhaar, where applicable), and 
category are correctly updated with their DPs.
21. The TDS rate applicable to the dividend shall be 
determined based on the shareholder’s residential status, 
valid PAN, eligibility for treaty benefits (for non-residents), 
applicable exemptions, if any, and submission of the 
requisite	information	and	documents	to	the	Company.
22. The Company will issue a separate communication to the 
Members outlining the applicable tax rates for various 
categories of shareholders and specifying the documents 
and	information	 required	 to	be	submitted.	 The	said	
details will also be available on the Company’s website at 
www.travelfoodservices.com.
23.	 Members	 are	requested	 to	submit	the	requisite	
documents and information to the RTA within the 
timelines prescribed in the aforesaid communication, 
to enable the Company to determine the appropriate 
rate	at	which	tax	is	required	to	be	deducted	 at	source	in	
accordance with the provisions of the IT Act.24. Procedure for remote e-voting for the AGM
a.  Pursuant to MCA Circulars and SEBI Circulars and other 
applicable circulars and notifications issued (including 
any statutory modifications or re-enactment thereof for 
the time being in force and as amended from time to 
time), companies are allowed to hold AGM through VC 
/OAVM, without the physical presence of Members at a 
common venue. In compliance with the said Circulars, 
AGM is being convened through VC/OAVM.
b. Pursuant to the Circular No. 14/2020 dated April 08, 2020, 
issued by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs, the facility to 
appoint proxy to attend and cast vote for the Members is 
not available for this AGM. However, the Body Corporates 
are entitled to appoint authorised representatives to 
attend the AGM through VC/OAVM and participate 
thereat and cast their votes through e-voting.
c. The Members can join the AGM in the VC/OAVM mode 
15 minutes before and after the scheduled time of the 
commencement of the Meeting by following the procedure 
mentioned in the Notice. The facility of participation at 
the AGM through VC/OAVM will be made available for 
1000 members on first come first served basis. This will 
not include large Shareholders (Shareholders holding 2% 
or more shareholding), Promoters, Institutional Investors, 
Directors, Key Managerial Personnel, the Chairperson 
of the Audit Committee, Nomination and Remuneration 
Committee and Stakeholders Relationship Committee, 
Auditors etc. who are allowed to attend the AGM without 
restriction on account of first come first served basis.
d. The attendance of the Members attending the AGM 
through VC/OAVM will be counted for the purpose of 
reckoning	the	quorum	under	Section	103	of	the	Act.
e. Pursuant to the provisions of Section 108 of the Act 
read with Rule 20 of the Companies (Management 
and Administration) Rules, 2014 (as amended)  the 
Secretarial Standard on General Meetings (SS-2) issued 
by the ICSI and Regulation 44 of SEBI Listing Regulations 
(as amended), and MCA Circulars issued from time 
to time the Company is providing facility of remote 
e-voting to its Members in respect of the business to be 
transacted at the AGM. For this purpose, the Company 
has	appointed	 NSDL	as	the	authorized	 agency,	for	
providing facility for voting through electronic means. 
The facility of casting votes by a Member using remote 
e-voting system as well as e-voting on the date of the 
AGM will be provided by NSDL.
f. In line with the MCA Circulars, the Notice calling the 
AGM has been uploaded on the website of the Company 
at www.travelfoodservices.com. The Notice can also 
be accessed from the website of the Stock Exchanges 
i.e. BSE Limited and National Stock Exchange of India 
Limited at www.bseindia.com and www.nseindia.com 
respectively and also available on the website of NSDL 
(agency for providing the Remote e-voting facility) i.e.  
www.ev oting.nsdl.com.

GENERAL INFORMATION AND INSTRUCTIONS FOR E-VOTING:
How do I vote electronically using NSDL e-Voting system?
The way to vote electronically on NSDL e-Voting system consists of “Two Steps” which are mentioned below:
Step 1: Access to NSDL e-voting system
A)  Login method for e-voting and joining virtual meeting for Individual shareholders holding securities in demat mode
 In terms of SEBI circular dated December 9, 2020 on e-voting facility provided by Listed Companies, Individual shareholders 
holding securities in demat mode are allowed to vote through their demat account maintained with Depositories and 
Depository Participants. Shareholders are advised to update their mobile number and email Id in their demat accounts in 
order to access e-voting facility.
 Login method for Individual shareholders holding securities in demat mode is given below:
Type of shareholders  Login Method
Individual 
Shareholders 
holding securities in 
demat mode with 
NSDL.1. For OTP based login you can click on https://eservices.nsdl.com/SecureWeb/evoting/evotinglogin.
jsp. You will have to enter your 8-digit DP ID, 8-digit Client Id, PAN No., Verification code and 
generate OTP . Enter the OTP received on registered email id/mobile number and click on login. 
After successful authentication, you will be redirected to NSDL Depository site wherein you can 
see e-voting page. Click on company name or e-voting service provider i.e. NSDL and you will be 
redirected to e-voting website of NSDL for casting your vote during the remote e-voting period or 
joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting.
2. Existing IDeAS user	can	visit	the	e-Services	 website	of	NSDL	Viz.	https://eservices.nsdl.com either 
on a Personal Computer or on a mobile. On the e-Services home page click on the “Beneficial  
Owner” icon under “Login”  which is available under ‘IDeAS ’ section , this will prompt you to 
enter your existing User ID and Password. After successful authentication, you will be able to 
see e-voting services under Value added services. Click on “Access to e-voting” under e-voting 
services and you will be able to see e-voting page. Click on company name or e-voting service 
provider i.e. NSDL and you will be re-directed to e-voting website of NSDL for casting your vote 
during the remote e-voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting.
3. If you are not registered for IDeAS e-Services, option to register is available at https://eservices.
nsdl.com.  Select “Register Online for IDeAS Portal”  or click at https://eservices.nsdl.com/
SecureWeb/IdeasDirectReg.jsp
4. Visit the e-voting website of NSDL. Open web browser by typing the following URL: https://www.
evoting.nsdl.com/ either on a Personal Computer or on a mobile. Once the home page of e-voting 
system is launched, click on the icon “Login” which is available under ‘Shareholder/Member’ 
section. A new screen will open. You will have to enter your User ID (i.e. your sixteen digit demat 
account number hold with NSDL), Password/OTP and a Verification Code as shown on the screen. 
After successful authentication, you will be redirected to NSDL Depository site wherein you can 
see e-voting page. Click on company name or e-voting service provider i.e. NSDL and you will be 
redirected to e-voting website of NSDL for casting your vote during the remote e-voting period or 
joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting.

Individual

CDSL1. Users who have opted for CDSL Easi / Easiest facility, can login through their existing user id and

The	users	to	login	Easi	/Easiest	are	requested	 to	visit	CDSL	website	 www.cdslindia.com and click 
on login icon & New System Myeasi Tab and then use your existing my easi username & password.
2. After successful login the Easi / Easiest user will be able to see the e-voting option for eligible companies 
where the evoting is in progress as per the information provided by company. On clicking the evoting 
option, the user will be able to see e-voting page of the e-voting service provider for casting your vote 
during the remote e-voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting. Additionally, 
there is also links provided to access the system of all e-voting Service Providers, so that the user can 
visit the e-voting service providers’ website directly.
3. If the user is not registered for Easi/Easiest, option to register is available at CDSL website  
www.cdslindia.com and click on login & New System Myeasi Tab and then click on registration 
option.
4. Alternatively, the user can directly access e-voting page by providing Demat Account Number 
and PAN No. from a e-voting link available on www.cdslindia.com home page. The system will 
authenticate the user by sending OTP on registered Mobile & Email as recorded in the Demat 
Account. After successful authentication, user will be able to see the e-voting option where the 
evoting is in progress and also able to directly access the system of all e-voting Service Providers.

B)  Login Method for e-voting and joining virtual meeting for shareholders other than Individual shareholders holding 
securities in demat mode and shareholders holding securities in physical mode.
 How to Log-in to NSDL e-voting website?
 1. Visit the e-voting website of NSDL. Open web browser by typing the following URL: https://www.evoting.nsdl.com/ 
either on a Personal Computer or on a mobile.
 2. Once the home page of e-voting system is launched, click on the icon “Login” which is available under ‘Shareholder/
Member’ section.
 3. A new screen will open. You will have to enter your User ID, your Password/OTP and a Verification Code as 
shown on the screen.
  Alternatively, if you are registered for NSDL eservices i.e. IDEAS, you can log-in at https://eservices.nsdl.com/ with your 
existing IDEAS login. Once you log-in to NSDL eservices after using your log-in credentials, click on e-voting and you can 
proceed to Step 2 i.e. Cast your vote electronically.
 4. Your User ID details are given below :
Manner of holding shares i.e. Demat 
(NSDL or CDSL) or Physical Your User ID is:
a)  For Members who hold shares in 
demat account with NSDL.8 Character DP ID followed by 8 Digit Client ID
For example if your DP ID is IN300*** and Client ID is 12****** then your 
user ID is IN300***12******.
b)  For Members who hold shares in 
demat account with CDSL.16 Digit Beneficiary ID
For example if your Beneficiary ID is 12************** then your user ID is 
12**************
c)  For Members holding shares in 
Physical Form.EVEN Number followed by Folio Number registered with the company
For example if folio number is 001*** and EVEN is 101456 then user ID is 
101456001***
 5. Password details for shareholders other than Individual shareholders are given below:
  a) If you are already registered for e-voting, then you can use your existing password to login and cast your vote.
  b) If you are using NSDL e-voting system for the first time, you will need to retrieve the ‘initial password’ which was 
communicated to you. Once you retrieve your ‘initial password’, you need to enter the ‘initial password’ and the 
system will force you to change your password.
  c) How to retrieve your ‘initial password’?
   (i) If your email ID is registered in your demat account or with the company, your ‘initial password’ is 
communicated to you on your email ID. Trace the email sent to you from NSDL from your mailbox. Open the 
email and open the attachment i.e. a .pdf file. Open the .pdf file. The password to open the .pdf file is your 
8 digit client ID for NSDL account, last 8 digits of client ID for CDSL account or folio number for shares held in 
physical form. The .pdf file contains your ‘User ID’ and your ‘initial password’.
   (ii) If your email ID is not registered, please follow steps mentioned below in process for those shareholders 
whose email ids are not registered.
 6. If you are unable to retrieve or have not received the “Initial password” or have forgotten your password:
  a) Click on “Forgot User Details/Password?”(If you are holding shares in your demat account with NSDL or CDSL) 
option available on www.ev oting.nsdl.com.
  b) Physical User Reset Password?” (If you are holding shares in physical mode) option available on  
www.ev oting.nsdl.com.
	 	 c)	 If	 you	are	still	unable	to	get	the	password	 by	aforesaid	 two	options,	you	can	send	a	request	at	evoting@nsdl.com 
mentioning your demat account number/folio number, your PAN, your name and your registered address etc.
  d) Members can also use the OTP (One Time Password) based for login casting the votes on the e-voting system of NSDL.
 7. After entering your password, tick on Agree to “Terms and Conditions” by selecting on the check box.
 8. Now, you will have to click on “Login” button.
 9. After you click on the “Login” button, Home page of e-voting will open.

Step 2: Cast your vote electronically and join General 
Meeting on NSDL e-voting system.
A.  How to cast your vote electronically and join General 
Meeting on NSDL e-Voting system?
i. After successful login at Step 1, you will be able to see all 
the companies “EVEN” in which you are holding shares 
and whose voting cycle.
ii. Select “EVEN” of company for which you wish to cast your 
vote during the remote e-voting period.
iii. Now you are ready for e-voting as the Voting page opens.
iv. Cast your vote by selecting appropriate options i.e. assent 
or dissent, verify/modify the number of shares for which 
you wish to cast your vote and click on “Submit” and also 
“Confirm” when prompted.
v. Upon confirmation, the message “Vote cast successfully” 
will be displayed.
vi. You can also take the printout of the votes cast by you by 
clicking on the print option on the confirmation page.
vii. Once you confirm your vote on the resolution, you will 
not be allowed to modify your vote.

not registered with the depositories for procuring 
User ID and password and registration of e mail ids 
for e-voting for the resolutions set out in this notice:

Consolidated Account statement, PAN (self attested 
scanned copy of PAN card), AADHAR (self attested scanned 
copy of Aadhar Card) to cs@travelfoodservices.com. If 
you are an Individual shareholders holding securities 
in	demat	mode,	you	are	requested	 to	refer	to	the	login	
method explained at Step 1 (A) i.e. Login method for

shareholders holding securities in demat mode.
2.	 Alternatively	 shareholder/members	 may	send	a	request	
to evoting@nsdl.com for procuring User ID and password 
for e-voting by providing above mentioned documents.
3. In terms of SEBI circular dated December 9, 2020 on 
e-voting facility provided by Listed Companies, Individual 
shareholders holding securities in demat mode are 
allowed to vote through their demat account maintained 
with Depositories and Depository Participants. 
Shareholders	 are	required	to	update	their	mobile	number	
and email ID correctly in their demat account in order to

To be the Best Part of Your Journey
Airports today are evolving into destinations in their own right, where 
customers increasingly seek convenience and comfort. At Travel Food Services 
Limited, our purpose has been to bring together great food, genuine hospitality 
and to be the best part of every traveller’s journey. We are proud of the 
platform we have built, underpinned by our leadership position in India’s 
Travel QSR and Lounge sectors, a strong portfolio of brands and enduring 
partnerships, which together provide a sustainable  
competitive advantage.
Building on these strengths, we continue to invest in customer-centric 
innovation, technology-enabled operations and disciplined expansion. Our 
focus remains on delivering differentiated experiences while enhancing 
efficiency and scalability. Through this balanced approach, we are 
strengthening our position as a leading travel hospitality company and creating 
long-term value for all stakeholders.

Our Market Debut  
Travel Food Services Limited (TFS) marked a significant milestone during the year with 
the listing of its equity shares on the BSE Limited and the National Stock Exchange of 
India Limited on July 14, 2025. The initial public offering, aggregating to approximately 
₹20,000 million, drew strong participation from a diversified investor base, reflecting 
confidence in our differentiated and scalable business model. The issue broadened 
the ownership, inviting both domestic and international investors to participate in the 
growth journey that we have been building over the years. The listing has strengthened our market position, increased 
our financial flexibility, and reinforced our commitment to 
governance and transparency. As we enter this next phase, 
we remain committed to building on this foundation, with 
a continued focus on delivering consistent performance. 
This will be driven by a balanced approach to growth and 
execution, supported by prudent capital allocation.

Who We Are
We are Travel Food Services Limited, India’s largest operator of Travel Quick Service 
Restaurant (Travel QSR) outlets and Lounges at airports. From a single outlet at 
Mumbai airport in 2009, we have grown into a multi-geography, multi-format food 
and beverage (F&B) enterprise, serving millions of customers in domestic and select 
international markets. As of March 31, 2026, we are present at 20 airports across India, 
Malaysia and Hong Kong, with a strong presence at India’s leading and fastest-growing 
airports.
For more than 17 years, we have worked to transform the way India eats on the move, turning the wait before a flight or the 
pause between connections into a moment of comfort and delight. Our portfolio of cuisines and brands is designed to address 
customer needs at every point of the journey, from a quick bite before boarding to a leisurely meal in a Lounge. This is a story of 
patience and purposeful growth, rooted in a close understanding of the traveller’s journey and a steady commitment to serving 
good food in one of the world’s most operationally complex environments for F&B.

At the heart of how we work are three guiding principles Ownership, Transparency & Trust, 
and People First. Together, our ‘OTP’ framework shapes the way we lead, the way we relate 
to one another and the culture we build across every QSR outlet and lounge we operate.

There are years that shape 
the arc of a company’s

was one such defining year

It gives me immense pleasure to present our first Annual 
Report as a publicly listed company. I am sincerely grateful to 
our shareholders, airport partners, bank partners, banking and 
card networks, regulators and customers for their continued 
support. Most importantly, I thank our employees, whose 
commitment and passion continue to power our growth.
There are years that shape the arc of a company’s journey 
and FY 2025-26 was one such defining year for TFS. Our listing 
marks not just a milestone in our journey, but also a deeper 
responsibility to operate with transparency, discipline and long-
term accountability to all stakeholders. It was also a year of 
meaningful results. Despite industry headwinds, we delivered 
strong performance, achieving growth across nearly every key 
metric we measure.
The Evolution of Airport Travel Hospitality
Before I turn to the year in detail, I want to reflect on how 
much airport hospitality has evolved. The biggest change I 
have witnessed has been in travellers' expectations of the 
airport experience. What was once viewed primarily as a transit 
point has become an integral part of the overall journey and 
hospitality has evolved alongside it, from a convenience-led 
offering into a category built around experience.
Travellers today expect greater choice, better food, more 
attentive service and experiences that feel personal to them. 
As those expectations have grown, so has the role of hospitality 
within the airport. It is now a category that rewards operators 
who can hold quality steady, at scale, across multiple locations 
with almost no margin of error. Through our Travel QSR and 
Lounge businesses, our aim is simple, to make the journey 
better with food and service that travellers genuinely enjoy.
One trend we have consistently observed is that traveller 
expectations tend to evolve faster than airport infrastructure 
itself. What differentiates successful operators is not simply 
their ability to respond to these evolving expectations, but 
their ability to anticipate them. This understanding continues 
to influence how TFS creates brands, designs customer 
experiences and invests in future capabilities.Dear Shareholders,
What Sets us Apart
While favourable industry dynamics provide a strong 
backdrop for growth, our confidence in the future is equally 
shaped by the capabilities we have built over time.
What sets us apart is the ability to combine airport operating 
discipline with hospitality capability by managing multiple 
brands, formats and service occasions in high-security, high-
footfall and operationally complex environments. We have 
built this capability on deep airport relationships, multi-brand 
execution, years of experience, and the ability to perform 
consistently at scale. Our business model is anchored in 
long-term master concession agreements and strategic joint 
ventures with leading multi-airport operators, providing us 
with a stable and enduring foundation for growth.
Together, these strengths create a platform that is 
difficult to replicate, thereby providing us with a strong 
competitive advantage.
India’s Aviation Industry: Long Runway Ahead 
India continues to be one of the most compelling aviation 
markets globally. As the world’s third-largest aviation market 
in calendar year 2025, passenger traffic is expected to grow 
between 7% to 8% annually over the next decade. The annual 
per capita air trips in India as per CY 2025 data were at 0.29 
compared to 1.08 in China and 5.59 in the United States, 
highlighting significant headroom for growth as rising incomes 
and increasing mobility are driving demand for travel.
The opportunity is also supported by sustained investments 
across the aviation ecosystem. Indian airlines have collectively 
placed orders for approximately 2,400 aircraft, nearly three 
times the current fleet size. Continued investments in new 
airports, terminal expansions and regional connectivity 
are strengthening the country’s aviation infrastructure and 
broadening access to air travel.
For businesses such as ours, the opportunity extends beyond 
passenger traffic growth alone. As traveller expectations 
evolve, businesses increasingly link value creation to customer 
engagement, premiumisation and the overall quality of the 
experience delivered. These trends align closely with our 
strengths and reinforce our confidence in the long-term 
opportunity ahead.
 
FY 2025-26: Highly Dynamic Year for the Indian 
Aviation Sector
During the year, the Indian aviation sector faced disruption 
from geopolitical developments, airline operational 
challenges and regulatory changes that affected flight 
schedules and capacity. These pressures weighed on 
passenger traffic at different points in the year and 
impacted overall Indian passenger traffic growth. 
At TFS-managed airports, passenger traffic remained  
broadly flat year-on-year (YoY) at a system-wide level.However, one of the key learnings from the year was the 
resilience of underlying air travel demand. While passenger 
traffic was impacted by temporary disruptions, recovery 
was swift whenever operating conditions normalised. This 
reinforces our belief that travel has become an increasingly 
ingrained part of both business and leisure consumption, 
supported by favourable demographic and economic trends.
For TFS, the year demanded agility, responsiveness and 
disciplined execution. Our teams navigated fluctuating 
passenger volumes, operational challenges and supply-chain 
disruptions while maintaining high standards of service across 
our network. This ability to execute consistently in a dynamic 
environment was one of the defining strengths of the year. 
For TFS, FY 2025-26 was a Year of Resilient 
Execution 
Against the backdrop of a challenging aviation sector, our 
performance was not merely a function of market growth; 
it reflected disciplined execution across contracts, brands, 
people, technology and operations.
During FY 2025-26, we continued to grow our network, 
broaden our portfolio and deepen our presence across key 
airports. Our airport network expanded to 20 airports at a 
system-wide level as we commenced operations at Cochin 
International Airport Domestic Terminal 1 and Navi Mumbai 
International Airport. These two strategically significant 
additions increase our footprints in India’s fastest-growing 
aviation corridors. At IGI Airport, New Delhi, we secured 
contracts across Terminal 1 and 2, enabling an integrated 
presence at India’s busiest airport. 
Overall, we added 76 Travel QSR outlets and two lounges at 
major airports during the year, bringing our total network 
to 518 Travel QSR outlets and 39 lounges. These additions 
reflected both successful mobilisation of new concessions and 
steady expansion across existing and new locations.
Equally important was the expansion of our brand portfolio. 
During the year, we increased our portfolio to 145 brands 
through a combination of international, regional and 
proprietary concepts. Additions such as Nando’s and Gordon 
Ramsay branded concepts enhanced our premium offerings

=== page 20 ===
Building for the Future: The EATS Platform
One of the most exciting developments during the year was 
the progress we made on the tech-enabled travel hospitality 
platform through our wholly owned subsidiary, Eliteassist 
Technology and Services Private Limited (EATS). During the year, 
EATS successfully deployed end-to-end integration with multiple 
banks and card networks, enabling seamless direct bank-to-
lounge access for travellers. This makes the lounge experience 
simpler, smoother and more accessible, while deepening our 
relationships with banking and payment partners.
Importantly, this is just the beginning of EATS' journey, laying 
the foundation for a broader travel hospitality platform. 
With a growing network of ecosystem partnerships, we see 
significant opportunities to expand its role across travel, 
hospitality and customer engagement, creating new avenues 
for growth and value creation in the years ahead.
Our People are Our Strength 
Our greatest strength is our people, who bring the TFS 
experience to life for every traveller, every day. The quality 
of that experience is a direct reflection of the commitment 
of our frontline teams operating in dynamic airport 
environments. Our guiding principles of OTP — Ownership, 
Transparency & Trust, and People First — run through every 
level of the organisation and guide how we think about and 
run our business.
We continue to invest in capability development through 
structured training and strive to build a more inclusive 
organisation across roles and locations. Our 32.5% female 
representation reflects our commitment to creating 
equitable opportunities and building a diverse workforce that 
strengthens the organisation.
Our efforts to build a strong, people-centric culture are 
earning external recognition. For the second consecutive 
year, TFS has been certified as a Great Place to Work, a 
distinction based on the voices and experiences of our 
employees. We were also honoured with the Most Admired 
Retailer of the Year 2025 – Employee Practices award at the 
MAPIC India Awards.
The strength of our business model was reflected 
in our financial performance. Growth was strong 
despite volatile passenger traffic, driven by 
like-for-like (LFL) sales growth and net contract gains.
System-wide sales reached ₹32,144 million, up 25.4% year-
on-year (YoY), supported by both LFL sales growth of 9.4% YoY 
and net contract gains of 13.1% YoY . LFL growth was driven 
by premiumised offerings and menu engineering initiatives, 
which form part of our continuous effort to refine our offerings 
in line with evolving customer choices. The net contract 
gains reflect expansion through the mobilisation of new units 
across locations.
Consolidated sales increased to J16,478 million and 
consolidated PAT increased to J4,523 million, reflecting 
adjusted year-on-year growth of 13.9% YoY and 21.5% YoY , 
respectively. Return on equity stood at 30.9%, while return on 
capital employed was 45.0%,  highlighting both the quality of 
earnings and the efficiency with which we deploy capital.
As of March 31, 2026, we remained debt-free and 
have sufficient liquidity to provide us with the financial 
flexibility and capacity to invest confidently in future 
growth opportunities.
System-wide sales reached ₹32,144 
million, up 25.4% year-on-year 
(YoY), driven by like-for-like sales 
growth of 9.4% YoY and net contract 
gains of 13.1% YoY .Building Sustainable Initiatives 
Sustainability is a core commitment for our Company. For us, 
sustainability is not separate from operations, it is embedded 
in how we source, serve, employ, train, reduce waste and 
support communities. Harnessing our local knowledge and 
supported by SSP’s global expertise, we have adopted an 
integrated sustainability strategy centred around three core 
focus areas: Product, Planet and People.  
We continue to expand our focus on responsible sourcing 
and healthier food choices for customers. This includes 
scaling our #Nourish brand, which is now present across 
airports, reflecting our commitment to promoting healthier 
consumption among travellers. We are also increasing the 
availability of healthier cuisine at our outlets and lounges, 
drawing on the inherent strengths of Indian food, which offers 
a rich variety of nutritious options.
On the environmental front, we remain focused on reducing 
waste across our operations, strengthening workplace safety, 
and transitioning towards more responsible material choices 
and sustainable packaging practices across our operations 
and supply chain.
Our CSR programme is anchored in positive social impact, 
with ‘Food and Nutrition for All’ as its central theme. Through 
our Foundation, we support community programmes 
spanning health and nutrition, livelihoods, environment 
and youth development, which tie to our philosophy of 
‘Prosper with Purpose.’
The Road Ahead 
Looking ahead, I remain confident in TFS’s growth trajectory. 
India’s aviation growth story remains firmly intact, and we are 
well positioned to capture the opportunity ahead. Our menu 
engineering initiatives, evolving brand mix, and investment 
in the traveller experience will support like-for-like sales 
growth. New unit mobilisations across multiple airports, such 
as Navi Mumbai, Cochin, Delhi, Noida, and Guwahati, will 
drive net contract gains in the near-term. Beyond our existing 
pipeline, we continue to bid actively for new opportunities at our existing and new locations. Additionally, through the 
continued evolution of EATS, we are deepening our service 
offerings within the airport hospitality ecosystem. 
Beyond airports, we are making early and disciplined 
investments in India’s expressway QSRs, which we see as a 
long-term growth opportunity. Internationally, our businesses 
in Malaysia and Hong Kong are performing well and we are 
actively exploring lounge opportunities across Asia-Pacific and 
the Middle East. 
With Gratitude
We extend our sincere gratitude to our Board for their 
guidance, our airport and brand partners for the trust they 
continue to place in us, our regulators for their ongoing 
support, and our shareholders for the confidence they 
have shown in the Company.  Their support remains 
fundamental to our ability to execute our vision and create 
long-term value.
India’s aviation sector continues to evolve, customer 
expectations are rising and new avenues of growth are 
emerging across travel hospitality. With a strong  operating 
foundation, trusted partnerships, a growing network and 
a committed team, we are entering this next phase with 
optimism and confidence. We look forward to building on 
this foundation responsibly and creating enduring value in 
the years ahead.
The best chapters of the TFS story are still ahead of us.
Warm regards,
Varun Kapur

Travel QSR Business We are India’s largest operator of Travel QSR outlets, serving travellers at the moments 
when they pause, refuel and recharge during their travel. Through our outlets, we 
offer a thoughtfully curated portfolio of food and beverage concepts spanning diverse 
cuisines, brands and formats. Each element is carefully curated to meet the unique 
demands of travel, where speed, convenience and quality matter most. Our network 
is primarily located within airports across India and Malaysia, complemented by a 
presence at select highway locations in India.

FY 2025-26: A Year of Meaningful Network Expansion and Premiumisation of Offerings 
As of March 31, 2026, our Travel QSR network had grown to 518 outlets on a system-wide basis, with 76 new outlets added 
during the year. This expansion spanned key airports, including Mumbai, Ahmedabad, Hyderabad, and the newly opened Navi 
Mumbai International Airport. We also operationalised outlets across newly awarded concessions, including Terminals 1 and 2 of 
IGI Airport, New Delhi and the Domestic Terminal of Cochin International Airport.  
Glimpses of Outlet Openings at Select Airports During FY 2025-26
• Setting-up 33 Travel QSR outlets at Terminal 1 and 14 outlets at 
Terminal 2 of IGI Airport, New Delhi
• Strengthens TFS’ footprint at India’s largest aviation hub with presence 
across all three terminals of the airportIndira Gandhi International (IGI) Airport, New Delhi – Terminal 1 & Terminal 2
• Among the top 10 largest airports in India and handled more than 11 
million passengers in FY 2025-26
• Establishing a portfolio of 11 Travel QSR outlets designed to cater to 
evolving customer tastesCochin International Airport – Domestic Terminal 1
• Operated through our Joint Venture, Semolina Kitchens Limited
• Bringing globally acclaimed and popular local brands to the airports 
• At Navi Mumbai International Airport, portfolio expansion is aligned 
with the long-term traffic ramp-up plans of the airport
• Operated through our Joint Venture, GMR Hospitality Limited 
• Setting-up Travel QSR outlets featuring a blend of in-house brands, 
international concepts, and regional favourites
• Growing presence at one of the key airports in South IndiaCSMIA Terminal 2 and Navi Mumbai International Airport
Rajiv Gandhi International Airport, Hyderabad
Way Forward
The opportunity ahead remains significant. With a large and diversified brand portfolio and a presence at many of the country’s 
busiest airports, we are well placed to capture the growth. Our focus will be on adding Travel QSR outlets and broadening our 
brand mix to match evolving customer requirements. With a well-matched format in each location and a sharper focus on menu 
innovation and service, we aim to increase spend per customer and turn rising footfall into higher revenues.

Lounge Business  
Our Lounge business operates the largest network of private airport Lounges in India, 
offering premium spaces within airport terminals that are thoughtfully designed to 
elevate the traveller experience. Since opening our first Lounge at Mumbai Airport 
Terminal 2 in 2014, we have increased our presence into multiple airports in India, 
serving millions of customers each year. Our Lounges serve travellers across all major channels, including first and business-class passengers, eligible credit and debit 
cardholders, loyalty programme members and walk-in guests. We collaborate with airlines, card networks and issuers, loyalty 
and lounge-access programmes and financial institutions, collectively referred to as our ‘Lounge Partners’ and generate revenue 
on a per-guest basis each time their customers access our Lounges.
At the core of our offering is a commitment to premium hospitality. Our Lounges combine thoughtfully curated food and 
beverage offerings, full-service bars and chef-led menus with a range of amenities designed to enrich the travel experience. 
These include spa and wellness services, shower and refresh facilities, local art displays and fully equipped workspaces, creating 
comfortable and welcoming environments where travellers can relax and recharge.

serve a wide spectrum of traveller segments and partner 
requirements, while maintaining consistent standards of 
quality and service across our lounge network.

International Lounges
Our International Lounges business represents the strongest 
expression of our premium airport hospitality capabilities 
and our ability to operate at globally benchmarked 
standards across diverse airport ecosystems.
Beginning with our entry into Malaysia and followed by our expansion into Hong Kong, 
we have built an international footprint of premium Lounges, supported by strong 
partnerships and deep operating capabilities. Today, we are among a limited group 
of operators with demonstrated capabilities across lounge design, development and 
operations across markets.
Countries with

OperationsInternational

International

2Scaling Success Beyond Borders
Malaysia
We entered Malaysia in 2022 in partnership with SSP and today 
operate 29 Travel QSR outlets and 8 Lounges across key airports 
at Kuala Lumpur, Kuching and Kota Kinabalu. Our Lounges 
serve first and business class passengers of partner airlines, 
alongside card programme members, corporate guests and 
walk-in travellers.
The Lounges are built to high standards, with spa 
centres, virtual golf simulators, gaming zones and a menu 
rooted in Malaysian hospitality. These operations have 
strengthened our ability to operate our lounge model 
in international markets and anchor our presence in an 
important Southeast Asian aviation market.
Hong Kong
Our entry into Hong Kong in July 2024 marked our arrival in 
one of Asia’s most demanding premium airport environments.  
The KYRA Lounge at Hong Kong International Airport (HKIA) 
was launched through SSP AD Lounges HK Limited, a joint 
venture between SSP TFS HK Lounge Limited and Airport 
Lounge Development Limited, a subsidiary of Airport 
Dimensions Holdings Limited.
KYRA, our premium lounge brand, is designed to support 
rest and recharge, paired with menus that blend distinctive 
local flavours with international favourites. In FY 2025-26, 
we launched our second KYRA Lounge at HKIA, reinforcing 
the strength of our proposition and our ability to scale 
successfully within a short period of entering a new market.
During FY 2025-26, we formed wholly owned subsidiaries 
in the UAE and Indonesia to pursue international Lounge 
opportunities. Our focus remains on the high-growth 
corridors of the Middle East and Southeast Asia, where 
rising passenger traffic and premiumisation trends present 
significant opportunity. We intend to leverage our proven 
operating expertise and deep understanding of the airport 
hospitality ecosystem to scale our global lounge footprint, 
replicating the service excellence and operational rigour that 
are the hallmark of our Lounges in India. Way Forward

=== page 30 ===
EliteAssist Technology 
and Services  
Technology is becoming increasingly central to how we serve travellers and FY 2025-26 
marked a significant milestone in our journey towards becoming a tech-enabled travel 
hospitality company. Under our wholly owned subsidiary EATS, we launched a dedicated 
technology platform that connects banks, card networks and enterprises directly 
with our airport Lounge network, providing a seamless experience for customers. The 
platform also enables differentiated customer propositions through technology-driven 
travel and hospitality solutions.(EATS)
Platform Advantages How EATS Works
EATS operates as a two-sided platform connecting a supply 
layer and a demand layer. By providing the technology 
integration, data intelligence, and commercial infrastructure 
required to bring these two layers together, EATS enables a 
direct, seamless and scalable ecosystem for lounge access.
EATS is already delivering meaningful advantages to our ecosystem partners. By providing unified access across our lounge 
network, it gives banks, card networks and partners a single point of integration making the experience faster and more reliable 
for both partners and passengers. 
The platform also enables us to deliver differentiated, value-added services within the lounge environment. Importantly, because 
EATS has been built as a scalable infrastructure layer rather than a standalone solution, it has the potential to extend well beyond 
lounge access and support a broader range of airport and travel-related services.
Banks & Card  
NetworkIntegrated 
Ecosystem ConnectivityDirect Lounge  
Access
In its first year, EATS has enabled 
direct bank-to-lounge access, giving 
customers a faster, more secure 
path into our Lounges, and the 
platform has stabilised well with 
an encouraging early response. As 
we look ahead, we will premiumise 
the experience and broaden the 
scope of services EATS supports, so 
that it continues to set the standard 
for how guests move through the 
travel hubs.

Designing Experiences 
Across the Customer 
Journey
Airports bring together travellers with diverse needs with some prioritising speed 
and convenience, while others seek comfort, discovery or indulgence. We design 
experiences around these moments through a combination of customer insights, 
personalised offerings, technology-enabled convenience and thoughtfully designed 
formats. By understanding traveller behaviours and preferences, we ensure that time 
spent at the airport is a more engaging, comfortable and memorable part of the 
journey.Menu Engineering and Innovation 
Our menu engineering approach is designed to grow sales along with improving the traveller experience. Using customer 
insights, consumption patterns and evolving travel occasions, we continuously refine our menus to improve conversion and 
encourage premiumisation across formats. A sharper product mix, curated bundles and premium offerings help us serve diverse 
customer needs while strengthening commercial performance. During FY 2025-26, we introduced several initiatives to capture 
incremental demand and improve the dining offer across our airport network.
 Breakfast Menu
New breakfast menu designed to capture morning 
demand Premium Beverage Range
Introduced differentiated beverage formats such as bottomless 
Coke, cold filter coffee and boba tea, offering travellers more 
choices
 Combo Meals
Value combo meals that appeal to price-conscious 
customers while supporting higher spend per visit
Speed & Convenience: Enabling Time-Sensitive Travel
Travellers under time pressure prioritise speed, clarity, and reliability. Our formats are built to enable quick decision-making 
and fast service without compromising quality. Simplified menus, self-ordering kiosks, and efficient layouts reduce wait times at 
every step and ensure easy access to food and beverages.
 Self-Ordering Solutions
Kiosks and digital ordering solutions that improve

People at the Core of 
Our Journey
Our people are what enable us to deliver consistent, high-quality hospitality, often 
under demanding operating conditions. Our teams balance speed, precision, culinary 
standards and reliable service, frequently at pace and under time pressure. Guided by

to nurture a culture built on accountability, collaboration and inclusion. Consistent, high-quality service across geographically dispersed airports and multiple formats demands a trained and adaptable 
workforce. Our training philosophy is built on the belief that capability-building is a continuous, structured and measurable 
process that must evolve with the needs of our business and our guests. We conduct multiple training programmes and have 
also invested in building leadership capabilities.

Our Responsible Growth 
Framework
Our ESG approach is structured around three interconnected pillars: Product, 
Planet and People. These pillars are supported by a strong governance 
framework ensuring clear oversight and accountability. Together, they drive 
continuous improvement in our sustainability and operational performance, 
shaping the experience we deliver to customers at every stage.We serve millions of passengers every day across India’s busiest

organisation in high-footfall environments, we consider every

What we source, serve and support defines how mindfully we operate

toward travellers, employees, communities, suppliers, airport partners

Product & Planet:  
Serving with Care
Environmental expectations within airport ecosystems are evolving rapidly. Airport 
operators increasingly prioritise energy efficiency and lower-impact operations, and 
place growing emphasis on improving waste management processes and practices across 
their partner networks. TFS has stayed ahead of this shift through proactive operational 
standards. As we grow, we continue to hold ourselves to high sustainability standards. PRODUCT PLANET
We maintain rigorous food safety, quality and hygiene 
standards across all operations. These standards are 
supported by regular audits and trained teams. We 
also source sustainable products and offer healthier, 
regional menu choices.Across our operations, we work to use resources efficiently 
and reduce our environmental footprint at every step. Our 
environmental focus areas include:
Energy Efficiency and Climate Action
We focus on improving energy efficiency through LED lighting, 
efficient equipment and selective use of renewable energy 
across our outlets, lounges and kitchens. Electricity and 
gas usage is monitored regularly to benchmark operational 
performance effectively. This helps identify inefficiencies and 
drive continuous improvement across all locations. Periodic 
electrical safety audits ensure safe, reliable, and compliant 
operational environments.
Healthier Menu Options 
We are introducing brands that promote wellness and 
nutrition as part of the overall brand proposition. Our 
#Nourish brand focuses on seasonal sourcing and promoting 
nutritionally rich food and beverages across airports.Promoting Local Sourcing
We prioritise local sourcing, with the vast majority of our 
direct product supply from Indian domestic suppliers. 
This supports Indian farmers, producers, and strengthens 
the domestic economy. We procure sustainably certified 
commodities wherever feasible across categories.
Procurement is governed through structured supply 
agreements and a supplier code of conduct. This framework 
integrates environmental expectations into all vendor 
engagement processes systematically. It promotes 
responsible sourcing, regulatory compliance and sustainable 
packaging adoption across suppliers.

Waste Management and Circularity
Managing waste across high-footfall airport ecosystems 
requires structured operational controls and continuous 
monitoring. Our waste management approach focuses on 
reducing food waste at source and improving efficiencies. 
We strengthen segregation and recycling practices while 
promoting circularity across operational by-products and 
ensuring responsible disposal across all locations.Plastic Reduction and Sustainable Packaging 
We use sustainable packaging and have replaced single-
use plastics with recyclable and compostable alternatives. 
We encourage reusable service ware at relevant customer 
touchpoints wherever feasible. We work with suppliers 
to reduce packaging waste across our operations and 
supply chain systems.

We implement conservation initiatives and install water

customer environments. Periodic reviews help identify 
efficiency improvements and strengthen water stewardship 
practices further.

Prosper with 
Purpose: Nourishing 
Communities
The power of food extends beyond hospitality and enables us to contribute 
meaningfully to the communities we serve. We believe that the same expertise, 
infrastructure and operational capability that serve millions of travellers each year can 
also help address some of India’s most pressing social challenges, including hunger, 
malnutrition and livelihood vulnerability. Through structured programmes, we work 
with communities and local partners to improve access to health, nutrition, and early 
childhood care for mothers and young children.
Our CSR vision 
‘Food and 
Nutrition for All’ 
Reflects our commitment to improving nutrition outcomes, 
strengthening food security and building resilient 
communities through scalable, food-linked interventions. 
We believe that access to nutritious food is fundamental to 
long-term health, dignity and opportunity.
Our Three Pillars of Impact
Our CSR programmes are structured around three interconnected pillars that collectively address immediate food access, 
long-term nutrition outcomes and livelihood resilience. Together, these pillars create a continuum of impact, from supporting 
vulnerable communities during critical life stages to strengthening household-level nutritional and economic resilience.
Addressing hunger lies at the core of our social impact 
efforts. Across India’s remote rural regions, food represents 
not only nourishment, but also a pathway to equity, 
education and dignity. We address immediate food insecurity 
through mid-day meals, food distribution initiatives and 
improved access to government welfare schemes, working 
alongside partners to bridge last-mile gaps and deliver 
nutrition to communities that need it most.Fighting Hunger and MalnutritionPillar 1

 Grain Donation Initiatives

partner organisations help address immediate nutritional
vulnerability among underserved households

Mid-day meals were provided to children studying in municipal 
schools through partnerships with implementation organisations
 Nurturing the First 1,000 Days
Focused interventions supporting pregnant women, lactating 
mothers, adolescent girls and children under the age of 2 
through nutrition education and maternal health awareness

Strong Governance shapes how we make decisions, manage responsibilities and 
create value over time. In a dynamic and highly regulated environment, we place 
strong emphasis on accountability, ethical conduct and transparency. Our governance 
approach helps maintain stakeholder confidence, strengthen risk oversight and support 
sustainable business growth.
We embed responsible business practices across our operations, partnerships and decision-making processes. Through clear 
governance policies, effective oversight led by the Board and committees, we safeguard stakeholder interests while maintaining 
operational discipline and resilience. As our business grows, our governance framework evolves to support responsible 
expansion and long-term value creation.Corporate Governance
 Our governance draws on a clear set of policies that shape how we operate and how our people conduct themselves each 
day. These frameworks translate our principles into practice, setting expectations for ethical behaviour, sound decision-making 
and fair dealing across the organisation. From the Code of Conduct that guides individual actions to policies governing risk, 
disclosure and related party transactions, each one reinforces accountability, protects stakeholder interests and upholds the 
standards we set for ourselves.
These policies give our teams a common reference point when judgement is called for, and bring clarity to expectations 
before decision-making. Responsibilities are mapped so that there is proper accountability and a clear route for taking a 
matter forward. Over time, this supports an environment in which issues are identified in time and addressed in a fair and

Your Directors have immense pleasure in presenting the 19th Annual Report of Travel Food Services Limited (formerly known as 
Travel Food Services Private Limited)  (the “Company” or “TFS ”), being the first Board’s Report following the listing of the Company’s 
equity shares pursuant to its Initial Public Offering. This Report provides a comprehensive review of the Company’s performance, 
together with the audited standalone and consolidated financial statements for the financial year ended March 31, 2026.

BUSINESS OVERVIEW AND OPERATIONAL PERFORMANCE
Your Company is a leading player in India’s fast-growing airport 
Travel Quick Service Restaurants (“Travel QSR”)  and Lounge 
sectors by revenue in Fiscal 2026, with a system-wide market 
share of approximately 30% in Indian Airport Travel QSR 
and 45% in India’s Lounge sectors, according to the CRISIL 
Report, 2026. The Company’s Travel QSR business comprises 
a diversified portfolio of food and beverage concepts across 
cuisines, formats, and brands, designed to cater to the 
requirements of travellers seeking speed, convenience, and 
quality in transit environments. The Lounge business provides 
premium hospitality services at locations within airport 
terminals, enhancing passenger comfort and experience. 
Your Company operates through a mix of partner and  
in-house brands, leveraging long-term relationships with global 
and domestic F&B brands, and benefits from the experience 
and support of its promoters, SSP Group plc and K Hospitality. During the year under review, your Company continued to 
strengthen its leadership position in the Travel QSR sector 
through expansion of its network and enhancement of its 
offerings. Your Company has established a strong presence 
across major travel hubs, with operations spanning India, 
Malaysia, and Hong Kong, and a network concentrated in some 
of the busiest airports handling a significant share of passenger 
traffic in India.
On a consolidated basis, revenue from operations for 
FY 2025-26 was J 16,477.96 million, as compared with  
J 16,877.39 million in FY 2024-25. This year-on-year movement 
reflects a change in the basis of consolidation rather than 
an underlying decline in the business. In FY 2024-25, SKL was 
treated as a subsidiary of the Company and its revenue was 
fully consolidated on a line-by-line basis until October 14, 2024, 
post which SKL is treated as a joint venture, and its results are 
no longer consolidated as revenue but are instead recognised 
within the Company’s share of profit of associates and joint 
Travel Food Services Limited  
76

=== page 51 ===
ventures. Therefore, on an adjusted basis that excludes SKL 
from the prior year, consolidated revenue from operations grew 
by approximately 13.90%, driven by like-for-like sales growth 
and net contract gains from the mobilisation of new units 
across key airports.
Profit before tax grew 18.07% to J 5,954.58 million and profit 
after tax grew 19.13% to J 4,523.17 million, with profit-after-tax 
margin improving to 27.40% (FY 2024-25: 22.50%). Profitability 
was further supported by a higher share of profit of associates and 
joint ventures of J 663.98 million (FY 2024-25: J 472.73 million), 
which includes the contribution from SKL following its 
reclassification, alongside faster mobilisation of units within 
the joint ventures. Consolidated basic and diluted earnings per 
share rose to J 33.49 and J 33.42, respectively compared to  
J 27.58 each in FY 2024-25.
On a standalone basis, revenue from operations grew 7.25% 
to J 13,021.42 million and profit after tax rose 22.56% to  
J 3,675.72  million.
The Company’s balance sheet remained strong. The Company 
continued to be debt-free, with a cash and investment balance of 
approximately J 8,355.69 million as at March 31, 2026, providing 
significant financial flexibility to fund growth opportunities and 
support long-term value creation. Return on capital employed 
and return on net worth stood at approximately 45.04% and 
30.90% respectively. 
A detailed discussion of the Company’s operational and financial 
performance is provided in the Management Discussion and 
Analysis Report, which forms part of this Annual Report.
During the year under review, there has been no change in the 
nature of the business of the Company.

INITIAL PUBLIC OFFERING AND LISTING OF EQUITY 
SHARES OF THE COMPANY
During the year under review, your Company marked a 
significant milestone with the successful completion of its 
Initial Public Offering (“ IPO”) and listing of its equity shares on 
BSE Limited and the National Stock Exchange of India Limited. 
The IPO comprised an offer for sale of 1,81,82,802 equity 
shares having a face value of J 1 each (at a price of  
J 1,100 per equity share) aggregating to approximately  
J 20,000 million by the promoter selling shareholder of the 
Company viz. Kapur Family Trust [Represented through its 
Trustee - SNVK Management Services Private Limited]. The 
equity shares were offered to the public through a book-
building process in accordance with applicable provisions 
of the Companies Act, 2013, the Securities and Exchange 
Board of India (Issue of Capital and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2018, and other applicable laws. The issue was 
opened for subscription on July 7, 2025 and closed on July 9, 
2025, and the equity shares were subsequently listed on the 
BSE Limited and the National Stock Exchange of India Limited 
on July 14, 2025. 
As the IPO was entirely an offer for sale, the Company did 
not receive any proceeds from the IPO. The proceeds were 
received by the promoter selling shareholder in accordance 
with the terms of the offer document. The listing of equity 
shares has enhanced liquidity for shareholders and facilitated 
wider investor participation in the Company.

Subsidiaries
a)  Eliteassist Technology and Services Private Limited

 EATS, a wholly-owned subsidiary of the Company, is

recorded revenue from operations of J 3,202.06 million.

Limited (“QMT”)
 QMT, a wholly-owned subsidiary of the Company, is 
engaged in operating food and beverage outlets, lounges 
and hospitality services across Noida International Airport. For the financial year 2025-26, QMT was yet to commence 
its full fledged business operations.

(“TFS Delhi T3”)
 TFS Delhi T3 is a subsidiary of the Company, in which 
the Company holds 60% of its shareholding and Delhi 
International Airport Limited holds the remaining 40% of 
its shareholding. TFS Delhi T3 is engaged in providing food 
& beverage and hospitality services at Terminal 3 of the 
Indira Gandhi International Airport, Delhi.
 For the financial year 2025-26, TFS Delhi T3 recorded 
revenue from operations of J 2,254.97 million, representing 
a 4.52% decline compared to the previous financial year.

Gurgaon”)
 TFS Gurgaon, a wholly-owned subsidiary of the Company, 
is engaged in managing and operating food and beverage

of J 292.57 million.

Global”)
 TFS Global is a wholly-owned overseas subsidiary 
incorporated in Mauritius and serves as the Company’s 
international investment holding and consultancy arm. 
The principal business of TFS Global is holding investments 
in the food and beverage services sector and providing 
consultancy services. 
 During the year under review, TFS Global continued to hold 
and manage the Company’s international investments 
and provide support for overseas business opportunities. 
TFS Global did not record any operating revenue 
during FY 2025-26.

 TFS Dubai, a wholly-owned overseas subsidiary 
incorporated in Dubai, is engaged in investment in 
commercial enterprises and management activities, 
including evaluating and pursuing business opportunities 
in international markets. TFS Dubai did not record any 
operating revenue during FY 2025-26.

incorporated in Indonesia, has been established to 
undertake restaurant and food & beverage operations,

opportunities in Indonesia. TFS Indonesia did not record 
any operating revenue during FY 2025-26.

SEPARATE MEETINGS OF THE INDEPENDENT DIRECTORS
Separate meetings of Independent Directors, as mandated 
under Schedule IV of the Companies Act, 2013 and Regulation 
25(3) of SEBI Listing Regulations are required to be held at 
least once a year to enable Independent Directors to (i) review 
the performance of non-independent directors and the Board 
as a whole, (ii) assess the performance of the Chairperson in 
terms of leadership and board effectiveness, and (iii) evaluate 
the quality, quantity, and timeliness of information flow 
from management to the Board. These provisions ensure 
that Independent Directors can meet without the presence 
Travel Food Services Limited

accountability, and overall corporate governance.

The Company had prior to its IPO formulated Travel Food 
Services - Employee Stock Option Plan (“ ESOP Plan ”), with an 
objective to reward the eligible employees of the Company 
and its group company(ies) including, subsidiary company(ies), 
holding company(ies) and / or associate company(ies), (present 
and future, if any), for their performance and to motivate them 
to contribute to the growth and profitability of the Company. The ESOP Plan aims to attract, retain and reward talent in 
the organisation. 
The Company had granted prior to the IPO 2,84,522 
Stock Options under ESOP Plan – Series 1 in terms of the 
resolution passed by the Nomination and Remuneration 
Committee on June 18, 2025. In terms of the ESOP Plan, 
25% of the stock options granted under the ESOP Plan – 
Series 1 have been vested for exercise during the current 
financial year 2026-27.
The ESOP Plan adheres to the provisions of the SEBI (Share 
Based Employee Benefits and Sweat Equity) Regulations, 
2021 (“ SEBI SBEB Regulations ”). In terms of the provisions 
of SEBI SBEB Regulations, the ESOP Plan was ratified by the 
Shareholders of the Company, vide resolution passed by the 
Members of the Company on September 24, 2025. 
A certificate issued by the Secretarial Auditor, certifying 
the implementation of the Company’s ESOP Plan, will be 
open for inspection by members at the forthcoming AGM.

The Company has established a framework of Internal 
Financial Controls designed to ensure accurate financial 
reporting, safeguard assets, maintain operational efficiency, 
and ensure compliance with applicable laws. These controls 
operate through well-defined policies and procedures, clearly 
laid-down authority matrices, and periodic internal audits that 
independently evalu ate their effectiveness. 
Key elements of the internal financial controls include: 
(i) structured reviews of operational and financial processes, 
(ii) ongoing monitoring of control gaps and timely 
remediation, and 
(iii) regular testing of design and operating effectiveness by 
the Internal Audit function. 
The Audit Committee provides oversight of this framework by 
reviewing internal audit reports, tracking corrective actions, 
and assessing the robustness of the control environment. 
Based on these reviews, audit findings, and management 
representations, the Board is satisfied that the Internal 
Financial Controls are adequate and operating effectively 
during the year under review.
RISK MANAGEMENT POLICY
The Company has implemented a comprehensive Risk 
Management Policy, which was approved by the Board, in 
accordance with Section 134(3) of the Companies Act, 2013 
and the SEBI Listing Regulations. The Policy establishes a 
structured and consistent framework for identifying, assessing, 
mitigating, and monitoring various risks that may impact the 
Company’s operations and strategic objectives.
Travel Food Services Limited  
82

=== page 57 ===
The Risk Management framework covers a wide spectrum 
of risks, including strategic, financial, operational, 
compliance, Environmental, Social and Governance (ESG), 
sectoral, reputational, information technology, and cyber-
security risks. 
In order to manage risks effectively across all business units 
and functions, a six -step process is implemented as part of risk 
management framework of the Company namely:
i. Risk Identification
ii. Risk Assessment
iii. Risk Prioritization
iv. Develop and Implement Risk Response
v. Develop Portfolio View
vi. Review and Monitoring
The Company’s Risk Management Committee, constituted 
in accordance with Regulation 21 of SEBI Listing Regulations, 
oversees the implementation of the Policy, periodically 
reviews the risk universe and mitigation measures, and 
updates the Board on key risk exposures and actions taken. 
The Company continues to integrate risk considerations into 
its decision-making processes to ensure business continuity, 
safeguard stakeholder interests, and enhance long-term 
value creation.

Annexure - III
Annual Report on CSR Activities of Travel Food Services Limited (“TFS”/”the Company”)
1. Brief outline on CSR Policy of the Company and the various initiatives undertaken by the Company: 
 The Company’s CSR Policy focuses on nutrition, healthcare, education, livelihoods, cultural preservation, rural development, 
and environmental sustainability, aligned with Schedule VII of the Companies Act, 2013. Through the K Corp Charitable 
Foundation and NGO partnerships, TFS implements community-focused initiatives. The “First 1,000 Days Programme ” 
supports maternal and child health, nutrition, and early development from conception to age two.
 i) Nutrition, Food Safety, and Sustainable Farming
  The programme addresses malnutrition among vulnerable groups through identification and management of 
malnourished children, promotion of breastfeeding and proper feeding practices, nutrition counselling, and regular 
growth monitoring. It encourages use of local nutritious foods, kitchen gardens, and food support for underserved 
communities, along with data-driven approaches to improve food quality, agriculture practices, and overall 
nutrition outcomes.
 ii) Healthcare and Medical Support
  The initiative strengthens maternal and child healthcare by ensuring early pregnancy registration, access to antenatal 
and postnatal care, screening of high-risk cases, and promoting institutional deliveries. It supports immunisation, child 
development monitoring, and effective referral systems. Additionally, it builds capacity of frontline health workers and 
improves coordination between health and Integrated Child Development Services systems to enhance service delivery.
 iii) Education, Livelihoods, and Women Empowerment
  The programme promotes awareness on health, nutrition, and early childhood development while strengthening 
community capacity through training of frontline workers and formation of support groups. It encourages women’s 
empowerment, gender equality, and male involvement in childcare. It also provides livelihood opportunities, 
life skills education for adolescents, and promotes informed decision-making, school readiness, and overall 
community development.

Management Discussion and Analysis
1 IMF World Economic Outlook Jul 2026 editionMacro-Economic Environment
Global Economy Overview
The global economy grew by 3.5%1 in CY 2025, similar to the 
previous year, according to the International Monetary Fund 
(IMF). Growth was broad-based, driven by a recovery in real 
wages and household purchasing power as inflation moderated 
from post-pandemic highs. Additionally, accelerating 
technology investments, mainly in artificial intelligence and 
semiconductors, along with resilient capital expenditure across 
key industries contributed to growth. 
However, growth dynamics remained divergent across regions. 
Advanced economies averaged 1.9%1 growth in CY 2025, led 
by the United States at 2.1%1, while the Euro Area lagged at 
1.4%1 amid weak industrial output and structural headwinds. 
Emerging Market and Developing Economies (EMDEs) 
continued to outperform at 4.5%1 growth, with China and India 
being the fastest growing economies. Global GDP Growth Trend (%)
Global private consumption strengthened in CY 2025, driven 
by easing inflation and improved income visibility, which 
helped stabilise household spending patterns. Central banks P – Projections

Source – International Monetary FundLooking ahead, IMF expects global growth to rebound to 
3.4% in CY 2027 (P). This recovery rests on a key assumption 
that traffic through the Strait of Hormuz returns fully to pre-
war conditions by March 2027. The balance of risks to the 
global growth outlook is skewed to the downside, given high 
uncertainty. A renewed escalation of the conflict could revive 
commodity price volatility and tighten financial conditions, 
thereby weighing on growth, mainly in low-income countries.
For central banks, price stability is expected to remain the 
priority, but there is no single prescription. The right course 
will differ from one country to another, shaped by the interplay 
of commodity prices, technology-led demand and the path of 
inflation expectations.pivoted towards monetary easing, which, alongside resilient 
labour markets, high savings buffers and improving consumer 
confidence, supported demand across major economies. A 
short-term but meaningful boost also came from households 
and businesses front-loading purchases of goods, especially 
durables, ahead of anticipated tariff-driven price increases. 
Taken together, these factors increased private consumption 
globally, though the gains remained uneven across regions 
and income groups.
Global Economic Outlook:  International agencies entered 
CY 2026 with relatively optimistic growth forecasts, but the 
outbreak of war in the Middle East disrupted this momentum. 
The closure of the Strait of Hormuz and severe damage to 
critical energy infrastructure have led to an increase in the 
prices of oil, gas, diesel and commodities. 
The IMF’s July 2026 World Economic Outlook update projects 
global growth of 3.0% for CY 2026 (P), observing that the 
global economy has held up better than feared following the 
war. A larger oil price spike was avoided through inventory 
drawdowns and an increase in oil production outside the Gulf 
region. Financial conditions tightened sharply in April 2026 
but have since eased and remain supportive by historical 
standards. However, the inflation forecast has been revised 
upwards to 4.7% for CY 2026 (P) from 4.1% in CY 2025, mainly 
due to higher energy and food prices.

Annual Report 2025-26
=== page 72 ===
FY 2025-26 was a landmark year for trade and diplomacy, 
with India expanding its Free-Trade Agreements (FTAs) to 38 
countries4. The standout was the India–EU FTA, which is set 
to increase exports and open access to 144 EU services sub-
sectors. A framework for an interim US-India trade agreement 
is expected to be finalised by 2026, reducing US tariffs on 
Indian goods ahead of a broader bilateral trade pact. Together, 
these deepen India's integration into global value chains and 
reinforce its position as a preferred trading partner across 
Europe, the Gulf, Asia-Pacific and North America. 
Indian Economic Outlook: Looking ahead, India is projected 
to sustain its position as one of the world’s fastest-growing 
major economies, with real GDP growth estimated at 6.6%2 for 
FY 2026-27 (P) notwithstanding anticipated spillover effects of 
the Middle East conflict on commodity prices and global trade. 
The moderation from the previous year reflects a normalisation 
of post-reform momentum and global headwinds as India’s 
structural fundamentals remain strong. 
India Inflation Trend (Consumer Price Index) (%)
On inflation, the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) now projects CPI 
to rise to 5.1%2 in FY 2026-27 (P), as the Middle East conflict has 
pushed up LPG, base-metal and other commodity prices. Food 
inflation could also firm as the conflict’s impact on fertiliser 
costs and shipping routes pushes prices above the base case 
and takes longer to wean off.
With risks now two-sided, the RBI is likely to hold its neutral 
stance and stay data-dependent, watching crude, the rupee 
and inflation expectations before resuming rate cuts. A 
sustained energy shock could delay further easing, while a 
contained outcome would preserve room to support growth.

Growth was broad-based across demand drivers. Private Final

health of the economy.

Global air passenger traffic trends and outlook (Billion)Aviation Industry Overview
Travel Food Services Limited (TFS) primarily operates Travel 
QSR outlets and Lounges at domestic and international airports 
and select highway locations. As such, the evolving dynamics 
of passenger traffic, airport infrastructure and premium travel 
demand are important for the company. The following section 
examines the key structural trends shaping global and Indian 
aviation market, the emerging Travel QSR and Lounge sectors, 
and the tailwinds driving TFS's long-term growth opportunity.
Global Aviation Industry Overview 
Air passenger traffic is a key indicator of economic development, 
and is closely linked to levels of economic activity and individual 
incomes. Post-COVID, global passenger traffic recovered 
steadily with the resumption of personal and business travel, 
reaching 9.4 billion6 passengers in CY 2024, the first year to 
surpass pre-pandemic levels. Traffic rose to 9.8 billion6 globally 
6 CRISIL Report 2026
7 WTTC’s latest Economic Impact Research (EIR)in CY 2025 and is projected to reach 10.2 billion6 in CY 2026 
(P), reflecting continued but more measured expansion as the 
sector moves beyond the post-pandemic surge. 
According to World Travel and Tourism Council (WTTC) 
estimates, the sector contributed USD 11.6 trillion7 to the 
global economy in CY 2025, accounting for 9.8%7 of global GDP , 
while supporting approximately 366 million7 jobs worldwide. 
Looking ahead, both global GDP and air passenger traffic 
are expected to record steady growth through CY 2030 (P),  
supported by increasing disposable incomes, expanding 
tourism activity, improving air connectivity and continued 
investments in aviation infrastructure. Rising travel demand 
across emerging markets and growing preference for air travel 
for both business and leisure purposes are expected to remain 
key growth drivers for the sector .

India is the world's third-largest aviation market and is growing at a fast pace. Air passenger traffic reached 420 million in FY 2025-
26 and is projected to rise to 785–815 million by FY 2034-35 (P), growing 3.6%-5.7%8 in FY 2026-27 (P), then at a 7.6%-8.6%8 
CAGR through FY 2030-31 (P) before moderating to 7.2%-7.6%8 CAGR by FY 2034-35 (P). Domestic passenger traffic continues to 
account for the majority of volumes, supported by strong intra-country connectivity and rising low-cost carrier penetration, while 
international traffic benefits from growing outbound travel, tourism and expanding global connectivity.Indian Aviation Industry Overview
